⏺ All the fixes we agreed on are in, plus the review-backend skill. 152 backend
  and 2 frontend tests pass and lint is clean. check-deploy still shows only the
  expected HSTS preload warning. Nothing is committed.

  Fixes

  Each fix started with a test that failed for the expected reason: 13 failing
  tests at the start, all passing now.

  #: 1
  Fix: The PUT/DELETE race is closed. Before writing, update and delete
    re-read the patient under a row lock, still limited to the requesting
    practitioner's patients. A PUT that loses the race gets a 404, and the
    update only saves the submitted fields, so it can't write active=True
    back. A second DELETE racing the first also gets a 404, the same answer as
    a DELETE sent after it.
  Where: patients/services.py, views.py
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 2
  Fix: Names with control characters (NUL included) are rejected with a 400 on
    Patient.name[0].family / .given.
  Where: patients/fhir.py
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 3
  Fix: Deep JSON no longer crashes. A JSON parser that turns RecursionError
    into a 400 is now the default everywhere, login included. FHIR input
    nested deeper than 10 levels is rejected before validation. Bodies over
    100 KB get a 413 with an OperationOutcome.
  Where: config/parsers.py (new), settings.py, fhir.py
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 4
  Fix: Unexpected errors return a generic 500 OperationOutcome. The log keeps
    the exception type and stack trace, but not the message, because library
    messages can contain submitted values.
  Where: patients/views.py
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 5
  Fix: Unsupported meta elements are rejected (meta.security, meta.tag…), no
    longer silently dropped. lastUpdated and versionId are still accepted and
    ignored.
  Where: patients/fhir.py
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 6
  Fix: The approximate login rate limit is documented as a README trade-off.
  Where: README.md
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: 7
  Fix: Tests added: <ul><li>test_services.py: the three races, replayed step
    by step.</li><li>test_selectors.py: patient scoping, tested on the read
    functions directly.</li><li>A 404 PUT/DELETE leaves the DB
    unchanged.</li><li>Deep JSON, body size, the 500 response, NUL,
    meta.</li></ul>
  Where: patients/tests/, accounts/tests/
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  #: Nits
  Fix: <ul><li>Uppercase UUIDs in the body id are
    accepted.</li><li>timezone.localdate() instead of
    date.today().</li><li>The management commands now go through
    accounts/services.py and accounts/selectors.py, so no ORM calls remain
    outside the allowed files.</li><li>The application code in the image is
    root-owned and the app user can't write to it.</li><li>.dockerignore now
    excludes .env* and the tests.</li></ul>
  Where:

  I replayed the reviewer's curl reproductions against the rebuilt stack: deep
  JSON on login, NUL and meta.security now get a 400, and an oversized body gets
  a 413. None return a 500 any more. I also built the production image: it runs
  as app, can't write to /code, and Django starts.

  I added two short rules to AGENTS.md matching the new code: the 500 handling,
  and "re-read under lock, save only the submitted fields".

  Skill

  .claude/skills/review-backend/SKILL.md follows what we did today:
  1. Hand the review to one fresh subagent, briefed with the code and the rules
     but not my opinion of the code.
  2. Relay its findings as a table ranked by severity, with my recommendation.
     Fix nothing yet.
  3. For each finding you pick: failing test first, then the fix, then replay
     the reviewer's reproduction.

  The brief inside it covers what this review checked: read-only, AGENTS.md as
  the reference, scope and priorities, proof for every finding, and the report
  format.

⏺ I created feat/app-claude-setup from the updated main, which includes your
  merge of the review fixes (PR #5).

  Plan: frontend agent instructions

  Files
  - app/AGENTS.md: the frontend reference, short.
  - app/CLAUDE.md: contains only @AGENTS.md.
  - Root CLAUDE.md: I'll move its "Frontend conventions" section into
    app/AGENTS.md and leave a link, so no rule is written twice (same as we did
    for the backend).

  app/AGENTS.md contents

  Section: Stack and layout
  Rules: <ul><li>src/api/: generated/ (Orval, never edited) plus client.ts,
    the single hand-written fetch.</li><li>One folder per feature: src/auth/,
    src/patients/.</li><li>src/ui/ for shared primitives.</li><li>Tests sit
    next to the code (*.test.tsx).</li></ul>
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Section: API
  Rules: <ul><li>Only the generated hooks and types.</li><li>fetch appears in
    exactly one place: client.ts, plugged into Orval as its custom fetch
    function (Orval's "mutator"). It sends credentials: 'include' and, on
    unsafe methods, the X-CSRFToken header read from the csrftoken cookie,
    fetching /api/auth/csrf/ first if that cookie is missing.</li><li>make
    schema after any API change.</li></ul>
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Section: Privacy
  Rules: <ul><li>No patient data in localStorage, sessionStorage, IndexedDB,
    console, URLs (no search terms; UUIDs only) or page titles.</li><li>The
    TanStack Query cache stays in memory, never persisted, and is cleared on
    logout and on 401.</li><li>The session cookie is the only auth: never
    read, store or send a token. The csrftoken cookie isn't a credential, it's
    the CSRF contract.</li></ul>
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Section: Errors
  Rules: <ul><li>401 → back to login, cache cleared.</li><li>Show FHIR
    OperationOutcome diagnostics, and map expression to the matching form
    fields.</li><li>Never show raw error text.</li></ul>
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Section: Lists
  Rules: TanStack Table (headless, so it fits Tailwind). Search, sorting and
    filtering are client side.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Section: Forms
  Rules: <ul><li>Native <form> plus Zod, preferring the generated
    schemas.</li><li>No form library: there are only two forms (login,
    patient), so react-hook-form isn't worth it.</li></ul>
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  Section: UI and accessibility
  Rules: <ul><li>Tailwind, mobile first, no component library: native elements
    cover what we need (<dialog>, <table>, <form>).</li><li>A label on every
    input, errors announced (aria-live), visible focus, full keyboard use, AA

    function (the "mutator") in orval.config.ts.
  - It sends credentials: 'include'.
  - On POST, PUT, PATCH and DELETE it adds X-CSRFToken, read from the cookie on
    every request (login rotates it). If the cookie is missing, it calls
    /api/auth/csrf/ first, once, even when several requests are waiting.
  - On a 4xx or 5xx it throws an ApiError with status and the parsed body. The
    error message is just "HTTP 404" and never contains the body, which may
    include patient data.
  - 12 new tests cover this. The app still loads and reaches the API through the
    proxy.

※ recap: We're building the patient app's frontend: its agent instructions, the shared fetch that handles cookies and CSRF, and the lint rules are done and passing on feat/app-claude-setup, but not committed. Next, you say whether to commit and push, and install frontend-design.

❯ Looks good. Where is the "401 → back to login" rule handled, and is it        
  tested?                                                                       
  Also, what happens if the CSRF token is no longer valid when I send a         
  request?                                                                      
  ⎿  1 skill available

⏺ 1. "401 → back to login": written down, but not implemented or tested yet

  Today the rule exists only as text in app/AGENTS.md. The code does half of it:
  - client.ts turns a 401 into ApiError(401), so the query or mutation fails
    with a clear status. That part is tested by the ApiError tests, with a 404
    example.
  - Nothing reacts to it yet. The query cache isn't cleared and no login screen
    is shown, because there is no login screen. That belongs to the auth screen
    step.

  Here's how I'd build it then, test first:
  - One handler for the whole app: the QueryClient gets an onError on both its
    query cache and its mutation cache. On ApiError with status 401, it calls
    queryClient.clear(), which drops every patient from memory, and the app
    shows the login screen. Individual screens never handle 401.
  - What triggers a 401: the 30-minute idle timeout, a logout in another tab, or
    a deactivated account. The backend returns 401 in each case, not 403: once
    the session is gone the request is anonymous, so DRF doesn't run its CSRF
    check.
  - Tests:
    - A patient list request answering 401 shows the login screen.
    - The cache is empty afterwards: no patient name left in the DOM.
    - The same holds when the 401 comes from a mutation.

  If you'd rather not leave a written rule with no code behind it, I can add the
  global handler now, with only a placeholder instead of the login screen.

  2. An invalid CSRF token

  Today: Django answers 403. On /api/patients/ that's an OperationOutcome
  forbidden with "CSRF token missing or invalid."; on /api/auth/* it's DRF's
  {"detail": "CSRF Failed: …"}. client.ts throws ApiError(403) and doesn't
  retry, so the screen would show a generic error. That behaviour isn't tested.

  How likely it is: low. In our setup the CSRF token lives only in the cookie,
  not in the session, and client.ts reads the cookie just before every unsafe
  request. So the header always matches the cookie the browser sends. A mismatch
  takes a race: another tab logs in and rotates the cookie between our read and
  the request leaving. A missing cookie isn't a problem, since we fetch a new
  one first. An expired session gives a 401, not a CSRF error.

  What I'd add (small, in client.ts, test first): on a 403 whose body says CSRF,
  call /api/auth/csrf/ to get a fresh cookie and retry once. A retry is safe
  even for a POST: Django rejects a bad CSRF token before the view runs, so
  nothing was written. A second 403 surfaces normally, so there's no loop. Other
  403s are never retried.

  Should I add the CSRF retry now? And for the 401 handler: now with a
  placeholder, or with the login screen?

⏺ The single CSRF retry is in app/src/api/client.ts, written test first. All 19
  frontend tests pass, and lint and typecheck are clean. The 401 handler stays
  for the login screen step, as agreed. Nothing is committed.

  What happens on a 403:
  1. If an unsafe request (POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE) gets a CSRF 403, the client
     calls /api/auth/csrf/ to get a fresh cookie, then sends the same request
     once more with the new token.
  2. If that second attempt fails too, the 403 is thrown as an ApiError, with no
     further retry.
  3. Any other 403, and any GET, is never retried.

  The two kinds of CSRF 403 are recognised by their bodies, which I checked with
  curl. On /api/auth/* it's {"detail": "CSRF Failed: …"}; on /api/patients/
  it's an OperationOutcome whose diagnostics read "CSRF token missing or
  invalid.". Django sends fixed messages in both cases.

  Tests: 5 new ones:
  - a retry for each body shape, checking the request body and the new token are
    sent again;
  - no second retry;
  - other 403s not retried;
  - GET not retried.

  That last test failed on my first version, which would have retried a GET. I
  fixed the code; the test was right.

  Checked on the stack: the retry relies on /api/auth/csrf/ sending a new cookie
  when the current one is invalid. It does: called with
  csrftoken=not-a-valid-token, it answers with a fresh Set-Cookie.

  I added a line about the retry to app/AGENTS.md so the doc matches the code.

  frontend-design is now installed, so I'll use it from the first screen. Ready
  to commit and push when you say.

⏺ I created feat/app-auth from the updated main, which includes your merge of
  the app setup (PR #6). I checked the generated code: the useLogin, useLogout
  and useGetMe hooks exist, along with a LoginBody Zod schema for the form.

  Plan: frontend auth

  New packages: react-hook-form, @hookform/resolvers, and
  @testing-library/user-event (AGENTS.md says tests act through user-level
  events).

  Files

  File: src/api/queryClient.ts
  Role: Creates the app's QueryClient (the shared cache for all API data).
    <ul><li>Global 401 handler: removes every cached query except /me, then
    re-runs /me, which gets a 401 and so shows the login screen. The /me query
    is excluded from this handler, so it can't loop.</li><li>No retries on
    4xx, so the login screen shows straight away.</li></ul>
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: src/auth/AuthGate.tsx
  Role: The guard. It calls /me when the app loads: <ul><li>loading → an
    accessible "Loading…" status;</li><li>401 → the login page;</li><li>any
    other error (500, network) → an error message with a "Try again" button,
    not the login page, since the session may still be valid;</li><li>success
    → the app.</li></ul>
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: src/auth/LoginPage.tsx
  Role: react-hook-form with zodResolver(LoginBody). <ul><li>On success the
    user is put into the /me cache, so no extra request.</li><li>Error
    messages:<ul><li>400 → "Invalid email or password."</li><li>429 → "Too
    many attempts. Wait a minute and try again."</li><li>anything else →
    "Something went wrong. Try again."</li></ul></li><li>The password field is
    cleared after a failure.</li></ul>
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: src/auth/LogoutButton.tsx
  Role: POSTs logout, then empties the cache with queryClient.clear(). /me
    re-runs, gets a 401, and the login page shows.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: src/App.tsx
  Role: Wrapped in AuthGate. The health check is replaced by a placeholder
    home: a header with the practitioner's name and the logout button, ready
    for the patient list.
  ────────────────────────────────────────
  File: src/test-utils.tsx
  Role: renderApp() using the real QueryClient, plus a fetch stub that answers
    by method + URL.

  Accessibility and privacy
  - Labelled inputs with autocomplete="username" and "current-password".
  - Errors are linked to their fields (aria-invalid, aria-describedby); the
    form-level error uses role="alert".
  - The first invalid field gets focus.
  - The button is disabled while the request is in flight and reads "Signing
    in…".
  - Nothing is stored or logged; the email lives only in the form state. No
    "remember me".
  - I'll design the login screen with the frontend-design skill: simple, mobile
    first.

  Small backend change: the login endpoint's OpenAPI schema currently documents
  only the 200 response. I'll add the 400 and 429 responses so the contract is
  accurate, then make schema.

  Tests, written first
  - Guard:
    - loading state;
    - 401 → login form;
    - /me succeeds → home with the name;
    - 500 or network error → error message + "Try again", and no login form.
  - Login:
    - empty or invalid email → field errors and no request sent;
    - success → home;
    - 400 → the generic message and the password cleared;
    - 429 → the rate-limit message;
    - network error → the generic message;
    - button disabled while the request is pending.
  - Logout: a POST to /api/auth/logout/, then the login form, and the cache is
    empty.
  - Session expiry: a later query answering 401 → login form, with the earlier
    data gone from the screen and the cache.

  Browser check at the end (Chrome):
  - log in as Alice;
  - wrong password → the generic message;
  - 6 attempts → the rate-limit message;
  - logout;
  - a reload while logged in stays logged in;
  - logging out in a second tab sends the first tab to login on its next
    request.

  OK with the plan?

⏺ Frontend auth works, both in tests and in your Chrome. All checks pass: 41
  frontend tests, 152 backend tests, lint and typecheck. Nothing is committed.

  What I built
  - The guard (src/auth/AuthGate.tsx) calls /me when the app loads:
    - loading → "Loading…";
    - 401 → the login form;
    - a server or network error → "We couldn't reach the server." with a "Try
      again" button;
    - success → the app.
  - The login page (src/auth/LoginPage.tsx) uses react-hook-form with
    zodResolver and the generated LoginBody schema, with user-friendly messages
    on top.
    - Every failed login shows one message, "Invalid email or password.", and
      clears the password.
    - Too many attempts show the rate-limit message.
  - Logout (src/auth/LogoutButton.tsx) ends the session and empties the cache.
    One change from the plan: if the logout request fails on the network, the
    server session still exists, so the app says "Couldn't log out. Try again."
    instead of pretending you're logged out.
  - One 401 handler for the whole app (src/api/queryClient.ts): any 401, from a
    query or an action, removes all cached data and re-checks /me, which shows
    the login form. It can't loop on /me. Client errors (4xx) are never retried.
  - Small backend change: the login endpoint's OpenAPI schema now documents the
    400 and 429 responses, and I regenerated it with make schema.
  - Design (with frontend-design): a calm pine and mist palette with AA
    contrast, system fonts so no third-party font request, a single left-aligned
    column with no card, and a pine bar at the top of the page as the one
    identity element.

  Tests (written first; 22 new):
  - the 401 handler, no loop on /me, and the retry rules;
  - the guard: loading, 401, valid session, server or network error with retry,
    and an expired session;
  - login: labels for password managers, success, empty or invalid email without
    a request, wrong credentials, 429, network error, and the button while
    pending;
  - logout: success with an empty cache, a network failure, and a 401.

  Checked in the browser:
  - Empty fields show their errors and focus the email field.
  - A wrong password shows the generic message.
  - The right password logs you in, and a reload keeps you logged in.
  - After a logout in a second tab, the first tab went back to login once it
    regained focus (/me → 401). The automation doesn't fire the browser's
    tab-focus event itself, so I fired it by hand to trigger this.
  - Six attempts: five 400s, then a 429 with the rate-limit message. That locks
    login from your machine for one minute.

  Worth knowing
  - localStorage on localhost:5173 holds one key, bonnie:done-conversations. It
    doesn't come from this app: another tool used the same address before. I
    left it alone.
  - I didn't check the narrow phone layout in the browser. The screens are a
    single column built mobile first, but I'll check them on a phone width when
    we build the patient list.

  Ready to commit and push when you say.

✻ Cogitated for 5m 12s · done 12:44 PM

❯ Why a separate routes.tsx instead of defining routes in App.tsx?              

⏺ Mostly convention, but there's one real constraint behind it.

  The constraint: the 401 handler has to redirect from outside React. A 401 is
  caught in the query client, not in a component, so it can't call
  useNavigate(). With createBrowserRouter, the router is a plain object, and
  main.tsx passes router.navigate('/login') to the query client. That means the
  route table has to exist as data that two places can build a router from:
  - main.tsx → createBrowserRouter(routes)
  - the tests → createMemoryRouter(routes, { initialEntries: ['/patients'] }),
    so they render the real routes at any URL.

  Why not App.tsx: an App.tsx usually holds a component. Here the thing being
  shared is an array, not something rendered, so routes.tsx says what the file
  contains.

  The alternatives:
  1. Keep the array, call the file App.tsx: export const routes = [...] in
     App.tsx. It works the same; only the name changes. Lint allows the constant
     export.
  2. Declarative routing in App.tsx: <BrowserRouter><Routes><Route 
     …/></Routes></BrowserRouter>, the classic react-router-dom style. The cost:
     there's no router object to hand to the query client, so redirecting on
     401 needs a small component inside the router that hands useNavigate() to
     the query client. That's an extra indirection, and the tests need their own
     wrapper.

  I'd keep the router created from the array (current setup or option 1),
  because the 401 redirect stays a single line in main.tsx. Whether the file is
  called routes.tsx or App.tsx is your call. Want me to rename it to App.tsx?

  list with TanStack Table (headless): global filter for the client-side search

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ We're on feat/app-patients. I checked the generated code and the backend's
  validation rules. One finding shapes the plan: the generated CreatePatientBody
  Zod schema checks the FHIR structure but not the API's full rules. It has no
  phone format, no email format, no check against future birth dates or control
  characters, and no "exactly one name". OpenAPI can't express most of those.

  Plan: patient page

  FHIR handling, in src/lib/patientFhir.ts: the only place that knows the FHIR 
  shape
  - Reading: a FHIR Patient becomes a flat PatientRow (id, family, given,
    birthDate, gender, phone, email): the official name[0], plus the phone and
    email picked out of telecom by system.
  - Writing: the form becomes a complete PatientRequest:
    - resourceType, name: [{use: 'official', family, given: [given]}], gender,
      birthDate;
    - telecom only for the fields that are filled;
    - id only on update (it must match the URL);
    - never meta or active, which the server manages.

    PUT replaces the whole resource, so clearing the phone removes it.
  - Server errors: each 400 issue expression maps to a form field:
    - Patient.name[0].family → family name, name[0].given → given name;
    - Patient.gender and Patient.birthDate → their fields;
    - Patient.telecom[i].value → phone or email, depending on which system we
      sent at index i.

    Anything else shows as a form-level error with its diagnostics.
  - Form schema (Zod, matching the API rules): it reuses the generated pieces
    (gender enum, the 150-character limits) and adds:
    - names: trimmed, required, no control characters;
    - birth date: a full YYYY-MM-DD, not in the future;
    - phone: optional, at most 32 characters, +/digits/spaces/().-;
    - email: optional, valid, at most 254 characters.

    Its tests reuse the backend's edge cases (NUL character, 151 characters,
    partial or future date, call me maybe). The server stays the authority.
  - Birth dates are formatted as local calendar dates, with no timezone shift
    (1980-05-17 must not show as 16 May), and the age is shown next to them.

  The list (pages/patients/), with TanStack Table (@tanstack/react-table,
  headless):
  - One row model: the global filter and the name sort feed one set of rows. A
    new useMediaQuery('(min-width: 768px)') hook in src/hooks/ decides which
    view to render, cards or table, never both. That keeps one copy in the
    accessibility tree and keeps the tests deterministic.
  - Search: over the full name and the patient ID. Case- and accent-insensitive:
    "zoe" finds "Zoë". The search text lives only in component state, never in
    the URL.
  - Sorting: by family name, then given name, with Intl.Collator. In the table,
    the column header button carries aria-sort; on mobile, a "Name A–Z / Z–A"
    button.
  - Columns or card lines: name with a short ID, birth date with age, gender,
    contact, and the actions "Edit" and "Stop following".
  - States:
    - loading → "Loading patients…";
    - error → an alert with "Try again";
    - no patients → "No patients yet" with an "Add patient" button;
    - no search match → "No patients match your search" with "Clear search".

  Dialogs: a native <dialog> with showModal(), wrapped once in
  src/components/Dialog.tsx. That gives focus trapping, Esc to close, an inert
  background, and focus back on the button that opened it. jsdom doesn't
  implement showModal, so test-setup.ts adds a small stand-in for it.
  - PatientFormDialog handles both add and edit. It uses react-hook-form with
    the form schema; the gender is a radio group and the birth date an <input 
    type="date" max={today}>.
    - After saving, the list refreshes and an aria-live message confirms the
      change.
    - On a 404 (the patient was archived, or you stopped following it
      elsewhere), the dialog closes with a message and the list refreshes.
  - ArchivePatientDialog: "Stop following Jane Doe?", explaining that the record
    is archived only if no other practitioner follows the patient. Buttons:
    "Stop following" and "Cancel". It sends the DELETE, then refreshes the list.

  Tests, written first
  - lib: reading and writing FHIR (create without id, update with id, an empty
    phone left out), the schema edge cases, and the error mapping (telecom index
    → field, unknown path → form).
  - Page:
    - loading, error with retry, empty;
    - sorted by name, sort reversed;
    - search by name (accent-insensitive) and by ID, and the no-match state;
    - cards vs. table depending on screen width.
  - Add: client validation blocks the request; the POST body has the right FHIR
    shape; the new patient appears; a server 400 lands on the right field; an
    unknown error shows at form level.
  - Edit: fields pre-filled; the PUT carries the id; clearing the phone removes
    it; a 404.
  - Stop following: Cancel changes nothing; confirming sends the DELETE and
    removes the row; a 404.
  - Accessibility: focus moves into the dialog, Esc closes it, and focus returns
    to the trigger.

  Out of scope: concurrent edits. The edit form starts from the list data, and
  the API has no optimistic locking, so the last write wins, as the README
  already documents.

  OK with the plan?

         +port changes. */                                               
       4 +export function useMediaQuery(query: string) {                 
       5 +  return useSyncExternalStore(                                 
       6 +    (onChange) => {                                            
       7 +      const media = window.matchMedia(query)                   
       8 +      media.addEventListener('change', onChange)               
       9 +      return () => media.removeEventListener('change', onChange
         +)                                                              
      10 +    },                                                         
      11 +    () => window.matchMedia(query).matches,                    
      12 +  )                                                            
      13 +}

⏺ The patient list is done on feat/app-patients-list: client-side search, sort
  by name, loading, error and empty states, cards on mobile and a table on
  larger screens. All checks pass: 71 frontend tests, 153 backend tests, lint
  and typecheck. Nothing is committed.

  A backend contract bug, fixed first. The generated type said the list returns
  an array of Bundles. drf-spectacular wraps every DRF list action that way, but
  our endpoint returns a single FHIR Bundle. I added a failing schema test,
  then fixed it in patients/views.py with a small custom schema class, the fix
  drf-spectacular documents. I regenerated the schema and the client: data is
  now one PatientBundle.

  What's built
  - src/lib/patientFhir.ts, the only code that knows the FHIR shape:
    - FHIR Patient → flat row: the official name, plus phone and email picked by
      system.
    - Birth dates are treated as calendar dates, with no timezone shift; tested
      with a Los Angeles timezone.
    - Age, short ID, accent- and case-insensitive search (every word must match,
      in any order), and a locale-aware name sort.
  - src/hooks/useMediaQuery.ts: picks the view, so only cards or the table is
    rendered, never both.
  - PatientList.tsx:
    - one TanStack Table row model (sort + global filter) feeds both views;
    - the search field has a label, and a polite live count: "Showing 1 of 4
      patients";
    - the search text stays in component state, never in the URL, and searching
      sends no request;
    - the Name header carries aria-sort, and the mobile sort button says what it
      will do;
    - in the table, the name is the row header.
  - PatientsPage.tsx:
    - "Loading patients…";
    - "Couldn't load your patients." with "Try again";
    - "No patients yet.";
    - "No patients match your search." with "Clear search".

  Things to know about the libraries
  - TanStack Table installed as v9, a new major with a new API. I followed the
    docs shipped in the package rather than v8 habits. Search and sort tests run
    with the React Compiler, so they would catch stale renders; they pass.
  - The linter caught a React Compiler issue: new Date() during render made the
    compiler skip the component. "Today" is now read once, when the list mounts.

  Tests (written first; 28 new):
  - lib: mapping, dates, age, search;
  - the hook;
  - page: loading, error with retry, empty, sort both ways, four kinds of
    search, nothing in the URL, no-match with clear, table vs. cards.

  stubApi now answers the patient list with an empty Bundle by default, since
  every signed-in page loads it.

  Browser check:
  - On desktop, Alice's 4 patients show sorted, and "brav" narrows the list to 1
    of 4.
  - Chrome refused to resize the window, so I checked the phone layout by
    loading the page in a 390 px-wide iframe: cards and the sort button render,
    with no table. At that width the header wraps onto two lines; I'll tidy it
    with the dialogs.

  The add, edit and "Stop following" buttons come on feat/app-patient-dialogs.
  Your empty feat/app-patients branch is still there locally; you can delete it.
  Ready to commit and push when you say.

  Compiler point matters here: a separate DataTable receives the same table

  pattern and the v9 type names first:

⏺ PatientList.tsx is split up. It went from 170 lines to about 70 and now only
  holds the table state and the choice between cards and table. All 78 tests,
  lint and typecheck pass, and the result works in the browser. This is on
  feat/app-patients-list, uncommitted.

  Shared, in src/components/:
  - SearchInput: a labelled search box with a "Clear search" button (×) that
    shows only when there's text. Clearing empties the box and puts the focus
    back in it; Escape clears too. The browser's own clear icon is hidden, so
    there's only one.
  - DataTable: renders any TanStack table.
    - Sortable columns get a header button and aria-sort; other columns get
      neither.
    - A rowHeader prop picks which column is rendered as the row's header cell.
    - It checks at runtime whether the table registers sorting, so it also works
      for tables without it.

  Patient-specific, in pages/patients/:
  - patientColumns.tsx: the TanStack features (name sort, name and ID search)
    and the column definitions.
  - PatientFields.tsx: BirthDate and Contact, shared by the columns and the
    cards. They're in their own file because React's fast refresh (live reload
    during development) needs a file to export only components.
  - PatientCards.tsx: the mobile view.
  - PatientList.tsx: the table state, the search box, the count, the mobile sort
    button, and the choice between views.

  Points worth knowing
  - React Compiler: the table object stays the same between renders, so a child
    given only table would never refresh. DataTable therefore takes rows as a
    prop, and its header subscribes to the sort state. A test covers this: new
    data on the same table instance shows up. I added the rule to app/AGENTS.md
    under "Lists", and listed the new components there.
  - Behaviour change: the no-match state no longer has its own "Clear search"
    button. The one in the search box replaces it, so two buttons with the same
    name never sit side by side.

  Tests: 7 new (4 for SearchInput, 3 for DataTable). The existing page tests
  pass unchanged.

  Ready to commit and push when you say.

⏺ The dialogs are done on feat/app-patient-dialogs: add, edit, and "stop
  following" with a confirmation. All checks pass: 116 frontend tests, 153
  backend tests, lint and typecheck. I also ran the whole flow in your browser.
  Nothing is committed.

  FHIR handling, in src/lib/patientForm.ts
  - Validation, matching the API's rules (reusing the generated gender list and
    150-character limit):
    - names: required, at most 150 characters, no control characters;
    - date of birth: a full, real date, not in the future;
    - gender: one of the four options;
    - phone and email: optional, phone +/digits/spaces/().- up to 32, email
      valid up to 254;
    - values are trimmed.

    The server still has the final say.
  - Form → FHIR: a complete Patient resource with the official name, gender and
    birth date, plus telecom entries only for contact details that are filled
    in. Because PUT replaces the whole record, clearing the phone removes it.
    meta, active and id are never sent; id is in the URL, and the generated
    request type doesn't allow it in the body anyway.
  - Server 400s → fields:
    - each OperationOutcome issue lands on its field by element path;
    - telecom[i] resolves to phone or email from what was sent at that position;
    - anything else becomes a form-level error.

  UI
  - components/Dialog wraps the native <dialog> modal:
    - keyboard focus stays inside the dialog and the page behind can't be used;
    - Escape and a Close button close it;
    - focus goes back to the button that opened it, or to the page heading if
      that button is gone.
  - components/TextField now has two users, so the login page uses it too.
  - The patient form dialog handles both add and edit:
    - the gender is a radio group and the birth date a date picker capped at
      today;
    - phone and email have autocomplete="off", so the browser can't fill in the
      practitioner's own details;
    - after saving, the list refreshes and the page announces "Jane Doe added."
      / "… updated.";
    - a 404 means the patient was archived or unfollowed elsewhere: the dialog
      closes with "This patient is no longer in your list." and the list
      refreshes;
    - a network error keeps the dialog open with your input.
  - The stop-following confirmation explains that colleagues keep access and
    that the record is archived, not deleted. Focus starts on Cancel, the safe
    choice. A failure shows a message inside the dialog.
  - Row actions: "Edit" and "Stop following" on table rows and cards. Screen
    readers announce them as "Edit Jane Doe", which starts with the visible
    word.
  - Header: it stays on one line on mobile; the practitioner's name truncates.

  Tests (written first; 38 new):
  - form rules, form → FHIR, and error mapping;
  - the dialog's focus and Escape behaviour;
  - each flow: success, client validation, a server 400 on the right field, 404,
    network error, cancel, the empty state, and mobile cards.

  Browser check (with a fake "Dialog Demo" patient):
  - add → listed;
  - edit (rename, clear the phone) → "No contact details";
  - stop following → gone from the list.

  That run caught one gap the tests missed: after "Stop following", focus fell
  back to the page body because the button that opened the dialog had left with
  its row. It now lands on the "Patients" heading, with a test. The test patient
  stays in the dev database as an archived record, hidden from the API.

  Worth knowing: LoginPage.tsx and routes.tsx use double quotes and semicolons,
  probably from your editor; the rest of the frontend doesn't. With no formatter
  configured for app/, I matched each file's style. Adding Prettier (or oxfmt)
  to make format would settle it. Should I add one in a separate commit?

  Ready to commit and push when you say.

⏺ Both changes make sense, and I'll keep two details:
  - The search count stays while you search ("Showing 1 of 4 patients"). It's
    the only feedback that the filter did something, and screen readers announce
    it.
  - The confirmation stays, with a label: "Last change: Jane Doe added." Without
    that line, a screen-reader user gets no confirmation after the dialog
    closes. The "no longer in your list" case after a 404 isn't a change, so it
    shows without the label. To keep that clean, the dialogs get a separate
    onGone callback instead of passing that message through onDone.

    read aloud.

⏺ The rule and the hook are both in place, and I proved the hook works in this
  session. All 117 tests, lint and typecheck pass. Nothing is committed yet.

  Pointer and focus rule
  - In index.css: every enabled clickable element now gets the pointer: buttons,
    links, role="button", summary, select, radios, checkboxes, and the labels
    wrapped around radios and checkboxes. The visible focus ring was already
    global through :focus-visible. In the browser, the gender radios, their
    labels and the dialog's close button now show the pointer; plain text-field
    labels keep the normal cursor.
  - In app/AGENTS.md (UI section): every clickable element shows the pointer and
    a visible focus ring. Never remove the ring (outline-none) on an
    interactive element, and use a real <button> or <a href>, never an onClick
    on a <div>.

  The hook
  - Config: .claude/settings.json gets a PostToolUse hook on
    Edit|Write|MultiEdit that runs .claude/hooks/check-app.sh. Your
    frontend-design plugin setting is kept. The file is shared in the repo, so
    anyone using Claude Code on it gets the hook.
  - What it does: for a file under app/ (generated code and node_modules
    excluded), it runs oxlint and tsc in the app container and reports both. On
    failure it exits 2, so the errors come back to me as a blocking message and
    I have to fix them. If the stack is down, it shows a "skipped" notice
    instead of blocking.
  - How I checked it:
    - Script on its own: a clean file passes, a backend file is ignored, and a
      probe file with a console.log and a type error is reported with both.
    - Live in this session: I wrote a probe file with a type error, and the
      error came straight back to me. I deleted the probe. The real index.css
      and AGENTS.md edits then passed silently.
  - Limit: the hook only sees the Edit/Write tools. Changes made with shell
    commands (I used some Python edits earlier) aren't checked. The note I added
    to app/AGENTS.md says to edit app/ files with Edit/Write. To close the gap
    entirely, a Stop hook could run the same checks at the end of each turn; I
    can add one.
  - Machine requirements: the script uses python3 (there's no jq on this
    machine) and Docker. Both are already needed for this project.

  You can review or disable the hook with /hooks. Ready to commit and push when
  you say.
